       weth.mint(borrower, 100e18);","newString":"        weth.mint(borrower, 500e18);","originalFile":"// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT\npragma solidity 0.8.28;\n\nimport {Test} from \"forge-std/Test.sol\";\n\nimport {BorrowMarket} from \"../src/BorrowMarket.sol\";\nimport {MockERC20, MockOracle} from \"./mocks/Mocks.sol\";\n\ncontract BorrowMarketTest is Test {\n    MockERC20 weth;\n    MockERC20 usdc;\n    MockOracle oracle;\n    BorrowMarket market;\n\n    address owner = makeAddr(\"owner\");\n    address lender = makeAddr(\"lender\");\n    address borrower = makeAddr(\"borrower\");\n    address liquidator = makeAddr(\"liquidator\");\n\n    uint256 constant ETH_PRICE = 2000e18; // $2000 / ETH\n\n    function setUp() public {\n        weth = new MockERC20(\"Wrapped Ether\", \"WETH\", 18);\n        usdc = new MockERC20(\"USD Coin\", \"USDC\", 6);\n        oracle = new MockOracle(ETH_PRICE);\n\n        market = new BorrowMarket(owner, address(weth), address(usdc), address(oracle), 0.05e18, 7000, 8500, 500, 5000);\n\n        usdc.mint(lender, 1_000_000e6);\n        usdc.mint(liquidator, 1_000_000e6);\n        weth.mint(borrower, 100e18);\n\n        vm.prank(lender);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        vm.prank(borrower);\n        weth.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        vm.startPrank(borrower);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n        vm.prank(liquidator);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n\n        vm.prank(lender);\n        market.supply(500_000e6, lender);\n    }\n\n    function _openPosition(uint256 collateral, uint256 debt) internal {\n        vm.startPrank(borrower);\n        market.depositCollateral(collateral, borrower);\n        market.borrow(debt, borrower);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n    }\n\n    function test_borrowUpToLtvAndNoFurther() public {\n        // 10 WETH @ $2000 = $20,000 collateral -> 70% LTV = 14,000 USDC\n        vm.startPrank(borrower);\n        market.depositCollateral(10e18, borrower);\n        assertEq(market.maxBorrow(borrower), 14_000e6);\n        market.borrow(14_000e6, borrower);\n\n        vm.expectRevert(BorrowMarket.PositionUnhealthy.selector);\n        market.borrow(1e6, borrower);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertEq(usdc.balanceOf(borrower), 14_000e6);\n        assertEq(market.debtOf(borrower), 14_000e6);\n    }\n\n    function test_interestAccrues() public {\n        _openPosition(10e18, 10_000e6);\n\n        vm.warp(block.timestamp + 365 days);\n\n        // 5% flat on 10,000 for one year.\n        assertApproxEqAbs(market.debtOf(borrower), 10_500e6, 1);\n        assertApproxEqAbs(market.supplyAssetsOf(lender), 500_500e6, 1);\n    }\n\n    function test_repayAndWithdrawCollateral() public {\n        _openPosition(10e18, 10_000e6);\n        vm.warp(block.timestamp + 30 days);\n\n        uint256 owed = market.debtOf(borrower);\n        usdc.mint(borrower, owed); // cover accrued interest\n\n        vm.startPrank(borrower);\n        market.repay(type(uint256).max, borrower);\n        assertEq(market.debtOf(borrower), 0);\n\n        market.withdrawCollateral(10e18, borrower);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertEq(weth.balanceOf(borrower), 100e18);\n    }\n\n    function test_withdrawCollateralBlockedWhenItBreaksLtv() public {\n        _openPosition(10e18, 14_000e6);\n\n        vm.prank(borrower);\n        vm.expectRevert(BorrowMarket.PositionUnhealthy.selector);\n        market.withdrawCollateral(1e18, borrower);\n    }\n\n    function test_healthyPositionCannotBeLiquidated() public {\n        _openPosition(10e18, 14_000e6);\n\n        vm.prank(liquidator);\n        vm.expectRevert(BorrowMarket.PositionHealthy.selector);\n        market.liquidate(borrower, 1000e6);\n    }\n\n    function test_liquidationSeizesRepayPlusBonus() public {\n        _openPosition(10e18, 14_000e6);\n        assertTrue(market.isHealthy(borrower));\n\n        // Drop ETH to $1600: collateral = $16,000, debt/collateral = 87.5% > 85%.\n        oracle.set(1600e18);\n        assertFalse(market.isHealthy(borrower));\n\n        uint256 repay = 5_000e6; // within the 50% close factor\n        uint256 wethBefore = weth.balanceOf(liquidator);\n\n        vm.prank(liquidator);\n        (uint256 repaid, uint256 seized) = market.liquidate(borrower, repay);\n\n        assertEq(repaid, repay);\n        // $5,000 * 1.05 / $1600 = 3.28125 WETH\n        assertEq(seized, 3.28125e18);\n        assertEq(weth.balanceOf(liquidator) - wethBefore, seized);\n        assertEq(market.debtOf(borrower), 9_000e6);\n        assertTrue(market.isHealthy(borrower));\n    }\n\n    function test_liquidationRespectsCloseFactorWhileSolvent() public {\n        _openPosition(10e18, 14_000e6);\n        oracle.set(1600e18);\n\n        vm.prank(liquidator);\n        vm.expectRevert(BorrowMarket.RepayExceedsCloseFactor.selector);\n        market.liquidate(borrower, 7_001e6);\n    }\n\n    function test_insolventPositionIsFullyClosedAndBadDebtSocialised() public {\n        _openPosition(10e18, 14_000e6);\n\n        // Crash below the debt: collateral = $13,000 < $14,000 debt.\n        oracle.set(1300e18);\n\n        uint256 supplyBefore = market.totalSupplyAssets();\n\n        vm.prank(liquidator);\n        (uint256 repaid, uint256 seized) = market.liquidate(borrower, type(uint256).max);\n\n        // Seizure clamped to the 10 WETH on hand; repayment recomputed from it.\n        assertEq(seized, 10e18);\n        assertEq(repaid, 12_380_952_380); // 10 * 1300 / 1.05, floored, in 6dp\n\n        (uint256 collateral, uint256 borrowShares) = market.positions(borrower);\n        assertEq(collateral, 0);\n        assertEq(borrowShares, 0, \"residual debt must be written off\");\n        assertEq(market.totalBorrowAssets(), 0);\n\n        // Lenders absorbed the shortfall.\n        assertLt(market.totalSupplyAssets(), supplyBefore);\n        assertApproxEqAbs(supplyBefore - market.totalSupplyAssets(), 14_000e6 - repaid, 2);\n    }\n\n    function test_lenderWithdrawLimitedByIdleLiquidity() public {\n        _openPosition(200e18, 400_000e6);\n\n        uint256 shares = market.supplyShares(lender);\n        vm.prank(lender);\n        vm.expectRevert(BorrowMarket.InsufficientLiquidity.selector);\n        market.withdraw(shares, lender);\n    }\n\n    function test_donationDoesNotMoveExchangeRate() public {\n        uint256 before = market.supplyAssetsOf(lender);\n        usdc.mint(address(market), 250_000e6);\n        assertEq(market.supplyAssetsOf(lender), before);\n    }\n\n    function test_repayIsAllowedWhilePaused() public {\n        _openPosition(10e18, 10_000e6);\n\n        vm.prank(owner);\n        market.pause();\n\n        vm.prank(borrower);\n        market.repay(1_000e6, borrower);\n        assertEq(market.debtOf(borrower), 9_000e6);\n\n        vm.prank(borrower);\n        vm.expectRevert();\n        market.borrow(1e6, borrower);\n    }\n\n    function test_constructorRejectsBonusThatBreaksThreshold() public {\n        // 8500 * 1.20 > 100% -> every liquidation at the threshold would create bad debt.\n        vm.expectRevert(BorrowMarket.InvalidParameters.selector);\n        new BorrowMarket(owner, address(weth), address(usdc), address(oracle), 0.05e18, 7000, 8500, 2000, 5000);\n    }\n}\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":28,"oldLines":7,"newStart":28,"newLines":7,"lines":[" ","         usdc.mint(lender, 1_000_000e6);","         usdc.mint(liquidator, 1_000_000e6);","-        weth.mint(borrower, 100e18);","+        weth.mint(borrower, 500e18);"," ","         vm.prank(lender);","         usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

weth;\n    MockERC20 usdc;\n    MockOracle oracle;\n    BorrowMarket market;\n\n    address owner = makeAddr(\"owner\");\n    address lender = makeAddr(\"lender\");\n    address borrower = makeAddr(\"borrower\");\n    address liquidator = makeAddr(\"liquidator\");\n\n    uint256 constant ETH_PRICE = 2000e18; // $2000 / ETH\n\n    function setUp() public {\n        weth = new MockERC20(\"Wrapped Ether\", \"WETH\", 18);\n        usdc = new MockERC20(\"USD Coin\", \"USDC\", 6);\n        oracle = new MockOracle(ETH_PRICE);\n\n        market = new BorrowMarket(owner, address(weth), address(usdc), address(oracle), 0.05e18, 7000, 8500, 500, 5000);\n\n        usdc.mint(lender, 1_000_000e6);\n        usdc.mint(liquidator, 1_000_000e6);\n        weth.mint(borrower, 500e18);\n\n        vm.prank(lender);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        vm.prank(borrower);\n        weth.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        vm.startPrank(borrower);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n        vm.prank(liquidator);\n        usdc.approve(address(market), type(uint256).max);\n\n        vm.prank(lender);\n        market.supply(500_000e6, lender);\n    }\n\n    function _openPosition(uint256 collateral, uint256 debt) internal {\n        vm.startPrank(borrower);\n        market.depositCollateral(collateral, borrower);\n        market.borrow(debt, borrower);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n    }\n\n    function test_borrowUpToLtvAndNoFurther() public {\n        // 10 WETH @ $2000 = $20,000 collateral -> 70% LTV = 14,000 USDC\n        vm.startPrank(borrower);\n        market.depositCollateral(10e18, borrower);\n        assertEq(market.maxBorrow(borrower), 14_000e6);\n        market.borrow(14_000e6, borrower);\n\n        vm.expectRevert(BorrowMarket.PositionUnhealthy.selector);\n        market.borrow(1e6, borrower);\n        vm.stopPrank();\n\n        assertEq(usdc.balanceOf(borrower), 14_000e6);\n        assertEq(market.debtOf(borrower), 14_000e6);\n    }\n\n    function test_interestAccrues() public {\n        _openPosition(10e18, 10_000e6);\n\n        vm.warp(block.timestamp + 365 days);\n\n        // 5% flat on 10,000 for one year.\n        assertApproxEqAbs(market.debtOf(borrower), 10_500e6, 1);\n        assertApproxEqAbs(market.supplyAssetsOf(lender), 500_500e6, 1);\n    }\n\n    function test_repayAndWithdrawCollateral() public {\n within the 50% close factor\n        uint256 wethBefore = weth.balanceOf(liquidator);\n\n        vm.prank(liquidator);\n        (uint256 repaid, uint256 seized) = market.liquidate(borrower, repay);\n\n        assertEq(repaid, repay);\n        // $5,000 * 1.05 / $1600 = 3.28125 WETH\n        assertEq(seized, 3.28125e18);\n        assertEq(weth.balanceOf(liquidator) - wethBefore, seized);\n        assertEq(market.debtOf(borrower), 9_000e6);\n        assertTrue(market.isHealthy(borrower));\n    }\n\n    function test_liquidationRespectsCloseFactorWhileSolvent() public {\n        _openPosition(10e18, 14_000e6);\n        oracle.set(1600e18);\n\n        vm.prank(liquidator);\n        vm.expectRevert(BorrowMarket.RepayExceedsCloseFactor.selector);\n        market.liquidate(borrower, 7_001e6);\n    }\n\n    function test_insolventPositionIsFullyClosedAndBadDebtSocialised() public {\n        _openPosition(10e18, 14_000e6);\n\n        // Crash below the uint256 seized) = market.liquidate(borrower, type(uint256).max);\n\n        // Seizure clamped to the 10 WETH on hand; repayment recomputed from it.\n        assertEq(seized, 10e18);\n        assertEq(repaid, 12_380_952_380); // 10 * 1300 / 1.05, floored, in 6dp\n\n        (uint256 collateral, uint256 borrowShares) = market.positions(borrower);\n        assertEq(collateral, 0);\n {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdUTtHb8AR1U7KaVASMhH","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01X6cuXNbWvrPrCjfEBkGCZN","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/Users/liana/Documents/prog/buidl-guidl/ethskills-evals/artifacts/security-goal-002/2026-07-28T122805Z-claude-no-skill-3/workspace/NOTES.md","content":"# WETH/USDC borrow market — notes\n\nThree contracts:\n\n| file | role |\n| --- | --- |\n| `src/BorrowMarket.sol` | the market: supply, borrow, repay, collateral, liquidation |\n| `src/ChainlinkPriceOracle.sol` | validated ETH/USD feed, normalised to 1e18 |\n| `script/Deploy.s.sol` | mainnet deployment with the intended parameters |\n\nDeployed parameters: LTV 70%, liquidation threshold 85%, liquidation bonus 5%,\nclose factor 50%, flat 5% APR.\n\n```\nforge build\nforge test\n```\n\n---\n\n## 1. How a position's health is computed\n\nA position is `{collateral (WETH wei), borrowShares}`. Two numbers drive everything.\n\n**Debt.** Interest is not stored per position. The market holds `totalBorrowAssets`\nand `totalBorrowShares`; a position's debt is its share of the first:\n\n```\ndebt = borrowShares * (totalBorrowAssets + 1) / (totalBorrowShares + 1e6)   // rounded UP\n```\n\n`accrueInterest()` runs first in every state-changing entrypoint and adds\n`totalBorrowAssets * rate * elapsed / (1e18 * 365 days)` to both `totalBorrowAssets`\nand `totalSupplyAssets`. Simple interest across each interval, compounding whenever\nthe market is touched. Any health check therefore sees debt as of the current block.\n\n**Collateral value**, quoted in USDC units so it can be compared to debt directly:\n\n```\nusd   = collateral * price / 1e18        // price = 1e18-scaled USD per whole WETH\nvalue = usd * 1e6 / 1e18                 // rounded DOWN\n```\n\n**The two thresholds.** They are deliberately different, and which one applies\ndepends on the direction of the action:\n\n| check | limit | applied by |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| LTV, 70% | `debt <= value * 7000 / 10000` | `borrow`, `withdrawCollateral` |\n| liquidation threshold, 85% | `debt > value * 8500 / 10000` ⇒ liquidatable | `liquidate` |\n\nAnything that *increases* risk must leave the position at or under 70%. Liquidation\nonly opens at 85%. The 15-point gap is the borrower's buffer against price moves and\naccrued interest; a position that could be opened at exactly the liquidation point\nwould be liquidatable in the same block.\n\nRounding is always against the position: debt rounds up, collateral value rounds\ndown. `healthFactor(user)` returns `(value * 85% / debt)` scaled by 1e18 — below\n`1e18` means liquidatable, `type(uint256).max` means no debt.\n\nNote this is a *price* health check, not a solvency guarantee. Between 85% and 100%\nthe protocol is still whole; past 100% the position is insolvent and liquidation\nproduces bad debt (§2).\n\n## 2. What a liquidator has to do\n\nCall `liquidate(borrower, repayAssets)`. Preconditions:\n\n- The market is not paused.\n- `debt > collateralValue * 85%` at the current oracle price, computed after interest accrual.\n- You hold `repayAssets` USDC and have approved the market for it.\n- `repayAssets <= closeFactor * debt` (50%) **while the position is still solvent**.\n  Once `collateralValue <= debt` the close factor is lifted and the whole debt may be\n  repaid, so the position can be closed out rather than left as a permanent hole.\n\nYou receive:\n\n```\nseized = repayAssets / 1e6 * 1e18 * 1.05 / price     (in WETH wei, rounded down)\n```\n\ni.e. the WETH-equivalent of what you repaid plus 5%. Passing\n`repayAssets = type(uint256).max` repays the maximum currently allowed.\n\n**The clamp — the part to actually watch.** If the requested repayment would seize\nmore WETH than the position holds, the seizure is capped at the collateral on hand\nand the *repayment is recomputed downward* from it. So the `repaid` you get back can\nbe less than the `repayAssets` you passed. Two consequences:\n\n- Simulate (`eth_call`) before sending, and encode your own slippage bound — the\n  contract does not take a `minSeized`/`maxRepaid` from you.\n- Both amounts move with the oracle price between simulation and inclusion. Price\n  updates are public; assume a competing searcher sees the same opportunity.\n\n**Bad debt.** If the clamp empties the collateral and debt remains, that remainder is\nunrecoverable. It is written off inside the same call: the borrower's shares are\nzeroed and the same amount is subtracted from `totalSupplyAssets`, so lenders take\nthe loss pro rata at that moment. Without this the exchange rate would keep counting\nmoney that will never arrive and the last lender to withdraw would eat the whole\nshortfall. `BadDebtRealised` is emitted — it is worth alerting on.\n\nLiquidation transfers collateral out *before* pulling USDC in, and the whole function\nis `nonReentrant`, so a liquidator contract can implement a flash-swap style callback\nin its `transfer` hook only if the collateral token has one — WETH does not.\n\n## 3. What an operator has to get right on mainnet\n\n### Ownership\n`owner` is set in the constructor and is `Ownable2Step`. It can set the oracle, set\nthe rate, and pause. **`setOracle` is effectively root** — a wrong price makes every\nposition liquidatable, and a liquidator draining the book is not reversible. Owner\nmust be a multisig behind a timelock long enough for lenders and borrowers to exit\n(48h+). Do not deploy with an EOA owner, not even \"temporarily\".\n\n### Oracle configuration\n`ChainlinkPriceOracle` wraps the mainnet ETH/USD feed\n(`0x5f4eC3Df9cbd43714FE2740f5E3616155c5b8419`, 8 decimals). Every read checks:\nanswer > 0, round completeness (`answeredInRound >= roundId`), `updatedAt` not in the\nfuture, age `<= maxAge`, and a min/max sanity band.\n\n- **`maxAge` against the real heartbeat.** That feed's heartbeat is 3600s; the deploy\n  script uses 4500s (one heartbeat plus slack). Set it too tight and the market\n  freezes on normal feed latency; too loose and you liquidate on a stale price.\n  Re-check the heartbeat at deploy time — Chainlink changes feed parameters.\n- **A stale feed makes `price()` revert, which halts borrowing, collateral\n  withdrawals and liquidations** — deliberately: it is better to stop than to act on\n  a wrong number. But it also means bad debt can accumulate through an outage. Have\n  an alert on feed age, not just on positions.\n- The min/max band (`$100`–`$100,000`) guards against an aggregator pinned at its own\n  circuit-breaker bounds. Set it wide; it is a tripwire, not a risk parameter.\n- This is a single feed with no fallback. A second source with divergence checks is\n  the obvious next hardening step.\n\n### Tokens\nAddresses in the deploy script are mainnet WETH and USDC — **verify them against a\nblock explorer before broadcasting**, do not trust them from this file.\n\n- USDC is an upgradeable proxy with a **blacklist and a global pause**. A blacklisted\n  borrower cannot receive borrowed USDC and a blacklisted lender cannot withdraw;\n  a USDC pause halts repayment and liquidation entirely, so positions can go\n  underwater with no remedy. This is unavoidable when USDC is the loan asset — know\n  it is there.\n- Decimals (18/6) are read from the tokens at deploy and stored immutably. Both are\n  fixed forever for these two tokens.\n- Neither token takes a transfer fee. The accounting assumes amount sent == amount\n  received; do **not** reuse this contract for a fee-on-transfer or rebasing asset.\n\n### Liquidity and seeding\nBorrowing draws from lender-supplied USDC. `availableLiquidity()` is\n`totalSupplyAssets - totalBorrowAssets`; lenders cannot withdraw past it, so at high\nutilisation lenders are stuck until borrowers repay. With a **flat** rate there is no\nutilisation feedback pulling the pool back toward liquidity — that is the main\nconsequence of keeping the rate model trivial, and the reason the rate is\nowner-adjustable. Watch utilisation and move the rate manually, or replace\n`ratePerYear` with a utilisation curve before this handles real size.\n\nSeed the pool with a real first deposit from an address you control. The virtual\nshares/assets offset (`1e6`/`1`) in `SharesMathLib` already makes the inflation\nattack uneconomic, but a non-trivial first deposit removes the question.\n\n### Parameter invariants (enforced in the constructor, worth understanding)\n- `ltv < liquidationThreshold < 100%`\n- `liquidationThreshold * (1 + bonus) <= 100%` — 8500 × 1.05 = 8925. If this were\n  violated, a liquidation triggered exactly at the threshold would immediately create\n  bad debt. Any future change to the threshold or bonus must preserve it.\n- `rate <= 100% APR`.\n\nLTV, threshold, bonus and close factor are **immutable**. Changing risk parameters\nmeans deploying a new market and migrating — that is the trade for not having a\nparameter-setter the owner could abuse. Decide before deploying.\n\n### Operational\n- Pause the market (`pause()`) on oracle trouble or a discovered bug. **Repayment is\n  intentionally exempt** so borrowers can always de-risk; liquidation *is* paused, so\n  a long pause during a falling market accrues bad debt. Pausing is a decision with a\n  cost, not a free safety switch.\n- `unpause()` accrues interest first, so a paused interval is not silently charged at\n  a rate set after the fact.\n- There is no minimum position size. Dust positions can become uneconomic to\n  liquidate at high gas and sit as small bad debt. Adding a minimum borrow floor is\n  cheap insurance if this goes live at size.\n- No upgrade path and no admin withdrawal — funds are only movable by the accounting\n  above. Verify the source on Etherscan after deploy.\n- There is no protocol fee: all interest goes to lenders.\n\n### Known limitations\nSingle collateral / single loan asset, one oracle, flat rate, no reserve factor, no\nfee switch, no dust floor, no flash-loan liquidation helper. Test suite here is a\nbehavioural smoke test, not an audit — invariant/fuzz testing and a review are\nprerequisites for real deployment.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":732,"cache_read_input_tokens":61993,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":732},"output_tokens":9,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"438f3f26-fd48-4d7a-a3b3-acacfba75153","uuid":"b37002b3-ed58-406d-acba-9156f5de6c80","timestamp":"2026-07-28T12:36:05.235Z","request_id":"req_011CdUTtGMxM7GZDJhh2QHE3"}

Liquidation\nonly opens at 85%. The 15-point gap is the borrower's buffer against price moves and\naccrued interest; a position that could be opened at exactly the liquidation point\nwould be liquidatable in the same block.\n\nRounding is always against the position: debt rounds up, collateral value rounds\ndown. `healthFactor(user)` returns `(value * 85% / debt)` scaled by 1e18 — below\n`1e18` means liquidatable, `type(uint256).max` means no debt.\n\nNote this is a *price* health check, not a solvency guarantee. What a liquidator has to do\n\nCall `liquidate(borrower, repayAssets)`. Preconditions:\n\n- The market is not paused.\n- `debt > collateralValue * 85%` at the current oracle price, computed after interest accrual.\n- You hold `repayAssets` USDC and have approved the market for it.\n- `repayAssets <= closeFactor * debt` (50%) **while the position is still solvent**.\n  Once `collateralValue <= debt` the close factor is lifted and the whole debt may be\n  repaid, so the position can be closed out rather than left as a permanent hole.\n\nYou receive:\n\n```\nseized = repayAssets / 1e6 * 1e18 * 1.05 / price     (in WETH wei, rounded down)\n```\n\ni.e. the WETH-equivalent of what you repaid plus 5%. Passing\n`repayAssets = type(uint256).max` repays the maximum currently allowed.\n\n**The clamp — the part to actually watch.** If the requested repayment would seize\nmore WETH than the position holds, the seizure is capped at the collateral on hand\nand the *repayment is recomputed downward* from it. Two consequences:\n\n- Simulate (`eth_call`) before sending, and encode your own slippage bound — the\n  contract does not take a `minSeized`/`maxRepaid` from you.\n- Both amounts move with the oracle price between simulation and inclusion. Price\n  updates are public; assume a competing searcher sees the same opportunity.\n\n**Bad debt.** If the clamp empties the collateral and debt remains, that remainder is\nunrecoverable. It is written off inside the same call: the borrower's shares are\nzeroed and the same amount is subtracted from `totalSupplyAssets`, so lenders take\nthe loss pro rata at that moment. Without this the exchange rate would keep counting\nmoney that will never arrive and the last lender to withdraw would eat the whole\nshortfall. `BadDebtRealised` is emitted — it is worth alerting on.\n\nLiquidation transfers collateral out *before* pulling USDC in, and the whole function\nis `nonReentrant`, so a liquidator contract can implement a flash-swap style callback\nin its `transfer` hook only if the collateral token has one — WETH does not.\n\n## 3. It can set the oracle, set\nthe rate, and pause. **`setOracle` is effectively root** — a wrong price makes every\nposition liquidatable, and a liquidator draining the book is not reversible. Owner\nmust be a multisig behind a timelock long enough for lenders and borrowers to exit\n(48h+). Do not deploy with an EOA owner, not even \"temporarily\".\n\n### Oracle configuration\n`ChainlinkPriceOracle` wraps the mainnet ETH/USD feed\n(`0x5f4eC3Df9cbd43714FE2740f5E3616155c5b8419`, 8 decimals). Every read checks:\nanswer > 0, round completeness (`answeredInRound >= roundId`), `updatedAt` not in the\nfuture, age `<= maxAge`, and a min/max sanity band.\n\n- **`maxAge` against the real heartbeat.** That feed's heartbeat is 3600s; the deploy\n  script uses 4500s (one heartbeat plus slack). Set it too tight and the market\n  freezes on normal feed latency; too loose and you liquidate on a stale price.\n  Re-check the heartbeat at deploy time — Chainlink changes feed parameters.\n- **A stale feed makes `price()` revert, which halts borrowing, collateral\n  withdrawals and liquidations** — deliberately: it is better to stop than to act on\n  a wrong number. But it also means bad debt can accumulate through an outage. Have\n  an alert on feed age, not just on positions.\n- The min/max band (`$100`–`$100,000`) guards against an aggregator pinned at its own\n  circuit-breaker bounds. Set it wide; it is a tripwire, not a risk parameter.\n- This is a single feed with no fallback. A second source with divergence checks is\n  the obvious next hardening step.\n\n### Tokens\nAddresses in the deploy script are mainnet WETH and USDC — **verify them against a\nblock explorer before broadcasting**, do not trust them from this file.\n\n- USDC is an upgradeable proxy with a **blacklist and a global pause**. A blacklisted\n  borrower cannot receive borrowed USDC and a blacklisted lender cannot withdraw;\n  a USDC pause halts repayment and liquidation entirely, so positions can go\n  underwater with no remedy. This is unavoidable when USDC is the loan asset — know\n  it is there.\n- Decimals (18/6) are read from the tokens at deploy and stored immutably. Both are\n  fixed forever for these two tokens.\n- Neither token takes a transfer fee. The accounting assumes amount sent == amount\n  received; do **not** reuse this contract for a fee-on-transfer or rebasing asset.\n\n### Liquidity and seeding\nBorrowing draws from lender-supplied USDC. `availableLiquidity()` is\n`totalSupplyAssets - totalBorrowAssets`; lenders cannot withdraw past it, so at high\nutilisation lenders are stuck until borrowers repay. With a **flat** rate there is no\nutilisation feedback pulling the pool back toward liquidity — that is the main\nconsequence of keeping the rate model trivial, and the reason the rate is\nowner-adjustable. Watch utilisation and move the rate manually, or replace\n`ratePerYear` with a utilisation curve before this handles real size.\n\nSeed the pool with a real first deposit from an address you control. The virtual\nshares/assets offset (`1e6`/`1`) in `SharesMathLib` already makes the inflation\nattack uneconomic, but a non-trivial first deposit removes the question.\n\n### Parameter invariants (enforced in the constructor, worth understanding)\n- `ltv < liquidationThreshold < 100%`\n- `liquidationThreshold * (1 + bonus) <= 100%` — 8500 × 1.05 = 8925. If this were\n  violated, a liquidation triggered exactly at the threshold would immediately create\n  bad debt. Any future change to the threshold or bonus must preserve it.\n- `rate <= 100% APR`.\n\nLTV, threshold, bonus and close factor are **immutable**. Changing risk parameters\nmeans deploying a new market and migrating — that is the trade for not having a\nparameter-setter the owner could abuse. Decide before deploying.\n\n### Operational\n- Pause the market (`pause()`) on oracle trouble or a discovered bug. **Repayment is\n  intentionally exempt** so borrowers can always de-risk; liquidation *is* paused, so\n  a long pause during a falling market accrues bad debt. Pausing is a decision with a\n  cost, not a free safety switch.\n- `unpause()` accrues interest first, so a paused interval is not silently charged at\n  a rate set after the fact.\n- There is no minimum position size. Dust positions can become uneconomic to\n  liquidate at high gas and sit as small bad debt. Adding a minimum borrow floor is\n  cheap insurance if this goes live at size.\n- No upgrade path and no admin withdrawal — funds are only movable by the accounting\n  above. Verify the source on Etherscan after deploy.\n- There is no protocol fee: all interest goes to lenders.\n\n### Known limitations\nSingle collateral / single loan asset, one oracle, flat rate, no reserve factor, no\nfee switch, no dust floor, no flash-loan liquidation helper. Test suite here is a\nbehavioural smoke test, not an audit — invariant/fuzz testing and a review are\nprerequisites for real deployment.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdUTyGHdXDDusuPRgvjKb","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Done. Foundry project builds clean and the 12 behavioural tests pass.\n\n**What's there**\n\n- `src/BorrowMarket.sol` — the market. Share-based accounting on both sides (Morpho-style virtual shares/assets, so donations can't move the exchange rate and there's no first-depositor inflation attack). Interest accrues into `totalBorrowAssets`/`totalSupplyAssets` at the top of every state-changing call. Debt rounds up, collateral value rounds down. `nonReentrant` + CEI throughout, `Ownable2Step`, pausable with repay deliberately exempt.\n- `src/ChainlinkPriceOracle.sol` — ETH/USD feed normalised to 1e18, reverting on non-positive answer, incomplete round, future timestamp, staleness past a configurable `maxAge`, or a price outside a sanity band.\n- `src/libraries/SharesMathLib.sol`, `script/Deploy.s.sol` (mainnet addresses + intended params), `test/BorrowMarket.t.sol`.\n- `NOTES.md` — health computation, liquidator flow, mainnet operator checklist.\n\n**Two design calls worth flagging**\n\nThe task describes only the borrow side, but borrowing needs a source of USDC, so I added a minimal lender side (supply/withdraw against shares, interest flows to lenders). Withdrawals are bounded by idle liquidity.\n\nLiquidation clamps: if the requested repayment would seize more WETH than the position holds, the seizure caps at the collateral on hand and the repayment is recomputed downward — so `repaid` can come back below what was passed in. Liquidators must simulate and set their own slippage bound; the contract takes no `minSeized`. When that clamp empties the collateral with debt remaining, the remainder is written off in the same call and subtracted from `totalSupplyAssets`, so lenders share the loss pro rata rather than the last one out absorbing it.\n\nRisk params (LTV, threshold, bonus, close factor) are immutable — changing them means a new deployment. The constructor enforces `threshold × (1 + bonus) ≤ 100%`, without which a liquidation at exactly 85% would instantly create bad debt.\n\nThe flat rate means no utilisation feedback: at high utilisation lenders are stuck until borrowers repay, with nothing pulling the pool back. That's inherent to keeping the rate model trivial as specified — it's why `ratePerYear` is owner-adjustable, and it's called out in NOTES.\n\nThe tests are a behavioural smoke test, not an audit — no fuzz or invariant testing."}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3992,"cache_read_input_tokens":67062,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":3992},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"438f3f26-fd48-4d7a-a3b3-acacfba75153","uuid":"8ff52f10-4ca4-476a-b1a9-0f1bd974b30b","timestamp":"2026-07-28T12:36:25.514Z","request_id":"req_011CdUTy7rshd1abrqx43y3Q"}
